Math Research Digest

Welcome to this week's *Grounded*, and what a week it is — the sheer density of long-standing problems falling in a single digest is almost disorienting. Perhaps most striking is the convergence of major breakthroughs in symplectic topology: a complete proof of the Arnold-Givental conjecture using integral Floer theory closes a decades-old problem about Lagrangian intersections, while a full classification theorem establishes that every once-extended 3D topological quantum field theory arises from the Reshetikhin-Turaev construction — together representing a remarkable settling of foundational questions that have organized the field for a generation. Readers who have been watching Koszul duality evolve will also want to look closely at this week's work lifting operadic duality to the (∞,2)-categorical setting, where a single equivalence between Morita categories of operads and cooperads finally unifies a constellation of previously scattered results under one clean framework. A second theme worth pausing on is the number of sharp threshold phenomena appearing across probability and combinatorics. The ellipsoid fitting paper identifies a critical threshold depending only on a single moment of the underlying distribution — a universality result whose elegance belies how hard such statements usually are to establish — while the queens-on-a-chessboard problem settles its conjectured formula ⌊4n

Number Theory

42

Modular periodicity of the Euler up/down numbers at odd prime powers

Berke Güleç

Euler up/down numbers count alternating permutations and, reduced modulo a prime power, eventually repeat periodically. Knuth and Buckholtz determined the period length modulo primes; this paper confirms Ramassamy's conjecture that their divisibility bounds for prime powers are actually equalities, pinning down exactly how the periodic structure of these combinatorial sequences behaves at higher prime powers.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

Let $E_n$ denote the number of alternating permutations of $\{1,\dots,n\}$, equivalently characterized by $\sum_{n\ge0}E_nz^n/n!=\sec z+\tan z$. For every $q\ge1$, the sequence $(E_n\bmod q)_{n\ge0}$ is eventually periodic; let $d(q)$ and $s(q)$ denote its minimal eventual period and preperiod. For every odd prime $p$, Knuth and Buckholtz proved $d(p)=\operatorname{lcm}(p-1,4)$ together with \[ d(p^r)\mid p^{r-1}d(p), \qquad s(p^r)\le r, \] and Ramassamy conjectured that both bounds are attained for every $r\ge1$. In this paper, we introduce an algebraic frequency expansion for the Euler numbers over $S_r=(\mathbb Z/p^r\mathbb Z)[x]/(x^2+1)$. Using Hurwitz series, the Euler sequence is represented algebraically as a finite combination of formal exponential modes, in a manner reminiscent of Fourier analysis. Using this expansion, we prove \[ d(p^r)=p^{r-1}d(p) \qquad \text{for every odd prime $p$ and every $r\ge1$}, \] thereby establishing Ramassamy's period conjecture. We also disprove the preperiod conjecture by proving \[ s(5^5)\le4<5. \] Finally, we prove that $5^5$ is the smallest odd prime power for which $s(p^r)\ne r$, and based on our findings we conjecture \[ s(p^r)\ge r-2 \] for every odd prime $p$ and every $r\ge2$.

Arithmetic purity of strong approximation for cubic hypersurfaces of large dimension

Chen Zhang

Can integer solutions to cubic equations be found 'almost everywhere' they should exist? This paper establishes 'arithmetic purity of strong approximation' for smooth cubic hypersurfaces — meaning local obstructions fully explain when global integer-like solutions fail — in dimensions at least 323 (or 30 over the rationals). Strong approximation is a key tool for understanding when local solutions (mod primes) lift to global ones, and pushing these dimension bounds lower is a central challenge in number theory.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this paper, we establish the arithmetic purity of strong approximation for smooth geometrically integral cubic hypersurfaces $X\subset \mathbb{P}^n$ over number fields $k$, provided that $n\ge 323$. For $k=\mathbb{Q}$, the bound can be lowered to $n \ge 30$.

Specialisations of the Burungale-Castella-Skinner main conjecture to $\mathbb Z_p$-lines

Ki-Seng Tan, Fabien Trihan, Kwok-Wing Tsoi

The Iwasawa main conjecture relates algebraic objects (Selmer groups of elliptic curves) to analytic ones (p-adic L-functions) over towers of number fields. This paper takes a known two-variable main conjecture and specializes it to every one-dimensional 'line' inside a Z_p²-extension of an imaginary quadratic field, producing precise characteristic-ideal identities that include an explicit local correction factor—refining the conjecture's predictions in each one-variable subfamily.

math.NT
Original abstract

Let $p>3$ be a prime, $E/\mathbb Q$ be an elliptic curve and $K$ an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the hypotheses of Burungale-Castella-Skinner, and let $L/K$ be the unique $\mathbb{Z}_p^2$-extension. In this note, by combining their integral two-variable main conjecture with the specialisation formula of the first-named author, we obtain a characteristic-ideal identity over every $\mathbb{Z}_p$-line in $L/K$, involving an explicit local factor. With the characteristic ideal of a non-torsion module defined to be zero, this identity also applies when the two-variable Perrin-Riou element specialises to zero. We call such lines exceptional and prove that only finitely many occur. We also prove that the cyclotomic line is non-exceptional with trivial local factor, and that the anticyclotomic line is exceptional. Finally, we bound the number of exceptional lines by the cyclotomic augmentation order and prove that if the $p$-primary Tate-Shafarevich group over $K$ is finite and the cyclotomic $p$-adic height pairing is non-degenerate, then this number is at most ${\rm rank}(E(K))$. In particular, when $E(K)$ has rank one, the anticyclotomic line is the unique exceptional line.

Torsion-vanishing for Siegel modular varieties via supercuspidal congruences

Ana Caraiani, Sug Woo Shin

When you compute the cohomology of Siegel modular varieties—spaces parametrizing abelian surfaces with extra structure—using torsion coefficients, where does it concentrate? This paper proves that the 'generic' part of this torsion cohomology lives strictly above the middle dimension, a result with implications for the Langlands program and p-adic Hodge theory. The method introduces a congruence technique to reduce to a supercuspidal setting where a trace formula becomes tractable.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove that the generic part of the cohomology of Siegel modular varieties with torsion coefficients is concentrated above the middle degree, under a suitable notion of genericity that is optimal in the unramified case. Our method relies on the semi-perversity of the relative cohomology of the Igusa stack and on a trace formula computation that is made possible by a congruence technique introduced by Scholze and Fintzen--Shin. Compared with the work of Yang--Zhu, which handles Shimura varieties of abelian type via categorical local Langlands, our method is adapted to Siegel modular varieties but handles coefficients of arbitrarily small characteristic.

$\mathbb{F}_q$-linear relations among Thakur's multiple zeta values in positive characteristic

Jinyuan Hu, Hanqing Huang, Li Lai et al.

Thakur's multiple zeta values are function-field analogs of classical multiple zeta values, living in positive characteristic arithmetic. This paper completely determines the dimension of the space spanned by these values at each weight over the finite field 𝔽_q, giving an explicit generating function, and constructs a concrete basis—resolving the full structure of 𝔽_q-linear relations among these mysterious transcendental quantities.

math.NT math.AG math.CO
Original abstract

Let $\mathcal{Z}_w^{(\mathbb{F}_q)}$ be the $\mathbb{F}_q$-linear subspace of $\mathbb{F}_q(\!(θ^{-1})\!)$ spanned by Thakur's multiple zeta values $ζ_A(\mathfrak{s})$ of weight $w$. We prove that $\sum_{w=1}^{\infty} \left(\dim_{\mathbb{F}_q} \mathcal{Z}_w^{(\mathbb{F}_q)}\right) x^w = \frac{x(1-x^q)(1-2x+x^q)}{(1-2x+x^{q+1})^2}$. Moreover, we construct an explicit $\mathbb{F}_q$-basis of $\mathcal{Z}_w^{(\mathbb{F}_q)}$, and prove that any $\mathbb{F}_q$-linear relation among Carlitz multiple polylogarithm values $\operatorname{Li}_A(\mathfrak{s})$ is an $\mathbb{F}_q$-linear combination of quadruple-carry relations. This result can be regarded as an $\mathbb{F}_q$-analogue of the corresponding $\mathbb{F}_q(θ)$-theorem proved by Chang--Chen--Mishiba and independently by Im--Kim--Le--Ngo Dac--Pham. Our discovery of the quadruple-carry relations is inspired by the recent work of Im--Kim--Ngo Dac. These relations may be viewed as $\mathbb{F}_q$-analogues of the double-shuffle relations among classical multiple zeta values $ζ(\mathfrak{s})$.

The k-Rank Taylor Family: Canonical Harmonic Lifts, Principal Parts, and Rademacher Series

Seokho Jin, Sihun Jo

Partition-theoretic rank statistics encode deep arithmetic information, but their generating functions—called Appell functions—resist standard modular-form classification because their Taylor coefficients span multiple weights. This paper shows that all non-holomorphic parts of these coefficients arise from a single weight-3/2 harmonic Maass form (a generalization of classical modular forms allowing mild non-holomorphicity), and produces explicit Rademacher-series formulas for their Fourier coefficients.

math.NT
Original abstract

For fixed $k\ge2$, Garvan's modified $k$-rank moments are encoded by odd elliptic Taylor coefficients of an odd-level Appell function. The completed coefficient of order $2n+1$ has weight $2n+3/2$, so the family cannot be realized as a fixed-weight vector-valued modular form. Nevertheless, all non-holomorphic parts arise from scalar contractions of canonical higher Serre derivatives of a single weight-$3/2$ vector-valued harmonic Maass form. Among lifts with the prescribed unary-theta shadow, the first $d=k-1$ Appell corrections are coordinates on the weakly holomorphic ambiguity and select a unique lift; the next correction is nonzero. The initial Taylor data recover the principal part without using positive Fourier coefficients of the modified moment series, and the principal part determines the shadow. The positive coefficients of the corresponding Maass--Poincaré lift have convergent Rademacher expansions; the canonical lift differs from it by a unique cusp form. This yields exact recursions for all even modified moments. For $k=2$, the cusp-form adjustment vanishes, giving an exact formula for the numbers of $2$-marked Durfee symbols involving a convergent Kloosterman--Bessel series.

Period Relations for Theta Products and Elliptic Integral Moments

Dianbin Bao

Why do certain integrals involving elliptic curves satisfy unexpected rational relationships? This paper uses period polynomials—algebraic objects encoding symmetries of modular forms built from theta functions—to give a unified, conceptual explanation for known but mysterious rational identities among elliptic integral moments. Previously these identities were found experimentally or by ad hoc analysis; the period-polynomial framework reveals they follow systematically from L-value relations.

math.NT
Original abstract

We develop a period-polynomial approach to relations among critical L-values of modular forms arising from theta products and elliptic integral moments. In particular, period relations provide a conceptual explanation for several known rational relations among moments of complete elliptic integrals that had previously been discovered through analytic or experimental methods.

A Neighbouring-Denominator Case of the Erdős--Mahler Conjecture

Diego Marques

The Erdős–Mahler conjecture says that if the largest prime factor of numerator-denominator products of a number's continued fraction convergents stays bounded, the number must be Liouville (transcendental and very well approximated by rationals). This paper proves a strengthened neighboring-denominator version: bounding the largest prime factor across three consecutive convergents suffices. The result makes progress on a 1939 conjecture using elegant classical tools from Diophantine approximation.

math.NT
Original abstract

In 1939, Erdős and Mahler conjectured that an irrational real number $ξ$ must be a Liouville number whenever $P(p_nq_n)$ is bounded for infinitely many convergents $p_n/q_n$, where $P(N)$ denotes the largest prime factor of a nonzero integer $N$. In this note, we prove a neighbouring-denominator case of their conjecture: if $P(p_nq_nq_{n+1})$ is bounded for infinitely many $n$, then $ξ$ is a Liouville number. The proof combines the determinant identity for consecutive convergents with a fixed-base estimate for linear forms in $p$-adic logarithms.

$k$-Lucas Annulus for Polynomial Zeros

Herbert Batte

The k-Fibonacci and k-Lucas sequences generalize the classical Fibonacci and Lucas numbers with a parameter k controlling growth. This paper proves a binomial identity connecting them whose form flips depending on whether a key index is even or odd—a parity phenomenon traced to a structural difference in their closed-form Binet formulas. As an application, the identity yields new annular regions in the complex plane guaranteed to contain polynomial roots.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove a binomial identity relating the $k$-Lucas and $k$-Fibonacci sequences: for real $k>0$ and integers $m\ge1$, $n\ge1$, a binomial-weighted sum of $k$-Lucas numbers reduces to a multiple of $L_{k,mn}$ when $n$ is even, but to a multiple of $F_{k,mn}$ when $n$ is odd. This parity dependence traces back to the $k$-Lucas Binet formula lacking the normalising factor $1/(α-β)$ present for $k$-Fibonacci numbers. The identity supplies, for each parity of $n$ and each $m$, an explicit family of positive weights summing to one; combined with the general annulus principle of Dalal and Govil, this yields a $k$-Lucas annulus containing all the zeros of a complex polynomial of degree $n$, complementing the $k$-Fibonacci-based bounds of Díaz-Barrero, Bidkham-Shashahani, and Kaur.

Discrete dynamics of Eratosthenes sieve

Fred B. Holt

The Sieve of Eratosthenes—the ancient algorithm for finding primes—can be recast as a dynamical system where each stage produces a structured cycle of gaps between surviving numbers. This paper shows that gap patterns (constellations) satisfying a span condition follow Markov chain statistics, giving precise probabilistic control over how prime constellations evolve through successive sieving stages.

math.NT
Original abstract

We study Eratosthenes sieve as a discrete dynamic system. At each stage of the sieve there is a cycle of gaps ${\mathcal G}(p^\#)$ of length $φ(p^\#)$ and span $p^\#$. There is a recursion ${\mathcal G}(p_k^\#)\longrightarrow {\mathcal G}(p_{k+1}^\#)$ that creates the next cycle from the current one. If we take initial conditions from the cycle ${\mathcal G}(p_0^\#)$, then for all constellations of span $|s| < 2p_1$, including gaps $g < 2p_1$, the driving terms of various lengths form Markov chains. These yield {\it exact} models for the populations $n_s(p_k^\#)$ for all further stages of the sieve. If $s$ is an admissible constellation of length $J$, then its population $n_{s,J}(p^\#)$ grows as $Θ\left( \prod (q-J-1)\right)$. So we factor out the superexponential growth to obtain the exact model for the relative population $w_s(p_k^\#)$ of the constellation $s$ across all further stages of the sieve. $$ w_{s,J}(p_k^\#) \; = \; n_{s,J}(p_k^\#) \, / \, \prod_{J+1 < p \le p_k} (p-J-1) $$ The asymptotic value of the relative population is a constant ${w_{s,J}(\infty) \ge 1}$ that depends only on the odd prime factors that divide a span in $s$. Assuming that the instances of a constellation $s$ are approximately uniformly distributed in ${\mathcal G}(p_k^\#)$, we develop first-order estimates of the number of instances $s$ that would occur in the interval of survival $ΔH(p_k) = (p_k^2, p_{k+1}^2]$. We define a statistic $η_s(p_k)$, the quadratic density of the constellation $s$ over the interval $ΔH(p_k)$. We show that the first-order estimates $\widehat{η_g}(p)$ for prime gaps agree with samples up to $5.677\,E14$.

Closed geodesics in homology classes modulo sublattices

Noam Pirani

On a random hyperbolic surface, closed geodesics (closed loops of locally shortest paths) can be sorted by their homology class—a topological label tracking how loops wind around the surface. This paper shows that as genus and a prime index q both grow, the count of geodesics in each class transitions from Poisson-distributed (sparse, random-looking) to Gaussian (bell-curve), depending on how fast the geodesic length X grows relative to q log X.

math.NT math.GT
Original abstract

Let $M$ be a Weil-Petersson random hyperbolic surface of genus $g$, and let $Γ\subset \mathbb{Z}^{2g}$ be a lattice of prime index $q$. We study the distribution of primitive closed geodesics in homology classes mod $Γ$ in the large genus limit. Averaging over all lattices of index $q$, with $q \to \infty$, we compute all the centered moments of the corresponding weighted counting functions, and exhibit a transition between Poisson and Gaussian regimes (depending on whether $\frac{X}{q\log X}$, the expected number of primitive geodesics in a given homology class mod $Γ$, tends to $λ>0$ or $\infty$). We also study the unnormalized variance $G_M(X,Γ)$ of the counts among homology classes, and show that as $X \to \infty$, averaged over all lattices of prime index $q$, it is asymptotic to $X\log X$ in the large genus limit. These results are analogous to phenomena arising in the distribution of primes in arithmetic progressions.

An explicit refined Gan--Gross--Prasad identity for Fourier--Jacobi periods of degree 2 Siegel cusp forms

Biplab Paul, Ameya Pitale, Abhishek Saha et al.

The Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture predicts deep relationships between periods of automorphic forms (special integrals encoding symmetry data) and central values of L-functions (generalizations of the Riemann zeta function). This paper computes previously unknown local integrals in the degree-2 Siegel cusp form setting and formulates an explicit identity linking Petersson norms of these forms to associated half-integral weight forms, with consequences for Fourier coefficient sizes and L-value non-vanishing.

math.NT
Original abstract

We compute the local integrals appearing in the refined Gan--Gross--Prasad conjecture for Fourier--Jacobi periods of $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ in new ramified cases and use this to formulate an explicit conjectural identity relating Petersson norms of degree 2 Siegel cusp forms and associated half-integral weight forms. We note consequences of our identity for the growth of Petersson norms, the size of Fourier coefficients, and non-vanishing of central $L$-values.

Introduction to the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer Conjecture

Fabio Ferrari Ruffino

The Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture — one of the Millennium Prize Problems — predicts that the number of rational points on an elliptic curve (a cubic algebraic curve with a group structure) is determined by the behavior of an associated analytic function called its L-function. This expository paper introduces the conjecture accessibly, defining both the algebraic rank (counting independent rational points) and analytic rank, and explaining why their conjectured equality remains one of mathematics' deepest open problems.

math.NT
Original abstract

We give a brief and partially informal introduction to the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture. After a short review on basic Diophantine equations, we define the natural abelian group structure on an elliptic curve, leading to the definition of the corresponding algebraic rank. Afterwards, we sketch how to define the analytic rank of an elliptic curve and we state the conjecture, that is, the equality of these two ranks.

The second moment of twisted modular $L$-functions and Dirichlet $L$-functions at the central point

Zhengye Chen, Yongxiao Lin

L-functions are complex-analytic objects encoding deep number-theoretic information, and their values at the central point s=1/2 are especially significant. This paper proves a precise asymptotic formula—with an explicit error bound—for the average squared magnitude of a product of two L-functions twisted by Dirichlet characters, assuming Selberg's eigenvalue conjecture. Such moment estimates are key tools for understanding how often L-functions vanish and the distribution of their values.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove an asymptotic formula with a power-saving error term for the second moment $$ \sum_{q \in \mathcal{Q}} \; \; \sideset{}{^\flat}\sum_{χ\bmod q} \left| L\big( \tfrac{1}{2} , χ\big) L\big(\tfrac{1}{2},f\otimes χ)\right|^2 $$ of the twisted ${\rm GL}(2)$ {$L$}-function and the Dirichlet {$L$}-function at the central point under the assumption of Selberg's eigenvalue conjecture. Here $f$ is a fixed Hecke holomorphic cusp form for $\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{Z})$ and the sum over $χ$ runs over all primitive even Dirichlet characters modulo $q$, and $$\mathcal{Q}=\left\{q=q_1 q_2\asymp Q: q_1 \leq Q_1, q_2 \leq Q_2,\ Q_2\asymp Q^{δ_1},(q,6)=1,(q_1,q_2)=1 \right\}$$ with $0<δ_1<0.0004$.

Intermediate-Range Estimates for Short Weyl Sums and Waring's Problem with Almost Proportional Summands

Karimjon Ibrohimjonovich Mirzoabdughafurov

Waring's problem asks how many k-th powers are needed to represent every sufficiently large integer as their sum; this paper tackles a constrained version where the summands must be nearly proportional to each other. The key tool is a new pointwise estimate for short Weyl sums—exponential sums that encode arithmetic structure—in a tricky intermediate range of rational approximation quality. With r = 2^n + 1 summands of degree n, the authors establish an asymptotic formula for the number of such representations.

math.NT
Original abstract

We obtain a uniform pointwise estimate for short Weyl sums of degree $n\geq3$ in the intermediate rational-approximation range $$ \frac{1}{qx^{n-2}y}\ll|λ| \ll\frac{1}{qy^{n-1}}. $$ This range arises from a second application of Dirichlet's rational approximation theorem in estimating the residual integral that occurs in the derivation of an asymptotic formula for Waring's problem with almost proportional summands. For $r=2^n+1$ and fixed positive numbers $μ_1,\ldots,μ_r$ satisfying $$ μ_1+\cdots+μ_r=1, $$ we derive an asymptotic formula for the number of representations $$ x_1^n+\cdots+x_r^n=N, \qquad |x_i^n-μ_iN|\leq H,\qquad 1\le i \le r, $$ valid for $$ N^{1-θ(n,r)+\varepsilon}\le H \le \frac{N}{\ln N}, \quad θ(n,r)= \frac{2}{n\bigl((r-1)(n-1)+2\bigr)}. $$ The resulting admissible lower bound for $H$ improves the previously known bound for every $n\geq3$.

The Mixed Second Moment of Quadratic Dirichlet $L$-functions with Prime Conductors

J. Macmillan

In the function field analogue of number theory, L-functions encode deep arithmetic information, and their moments reveal statistical behavior. This paper computes an asymptotic formula for a 'mixed second moment'—an average involving both an L-function and its second derivative—over prime polynomials. Such moment calculations are notoriously hard and help verify predictions from random matrix theory about L-function distributions.

math.NT
Original abstract

We establish an asymptotic formula for the mixed second moment involving the second derivative of the completed $L$-function, when averaged over monic, irreducible polynomials in the function field setting.

A Short Proof of a Conjecture Regarding Quadratic Representations of Practical Numbers

Ting Hon Stanford Li

A practical number is a positive integer where every smaller positive integer can be written as a sum of distinct divisors — a property analogous to powers of 2. This paper proves the second part of a conjecture by Wang and Sun, confirming that practical numbers always appear in certain quadratic representations involving prescribed odd and even parameters. The short proof resolves an open problem connecting additive number theory with the structure of practical numbers.

math.NT
Original abstract

A positive integer $n$ is called a practical number if every positive integer less than or equal to $n$ can be expressed as a sum of distinct positive divisors of $n$. In this paper, we study an open conjecture proposed by Wang and Sun concerning the quadratic representations of practical numbers. Specifically, we provide a short proof of the second part of the conjecture, demonstrating that for any positive integers $b$ and $c$ with $2 \nmid b$ and $2 \mid c$, there exists an integer $n$ satisfying $1 < n \le \max\{b, c\}$ such that $n^2 + bn + c$ is a practical number. Combined with the work of Somu, Li, and Kukla, this completely settles the conjecture of Wang and Sun.

Arithmetic theta invariants and arithmetic equilibrium measures

Mounir Hajli

Can the classical notion of an equilibrium measure—a canonical probability distribution minimizing energy, central to potential theory and complex geometry—be extended to the arithmetic setting where geometry meets number theory? This paper introduces arithmetic analogues of the Bergman distortion function (which measures how well holomorphic sections approximate a metric) on arithmetic varieties and uses them to construct a natural arithmetic equilibrium measure. The result opens a path to arithmetic analogues of classical results in pluripotential theory.

math.NT math.AG math.CV
Original abstract

We introduce two new arithmetic invariants associated with Hermitian line bundles on arithmetic varieties, which play the role of the Bergman distortion function in the arithmetic setting. As a first step, we show that these two functions are asymptotically equivalent. This leads to the introduction of an intrinsic measure $ \widehatμ^{\sup}_{\mathrm{eq}},$ which we show to be a natural arithmetic analogue of the equilibrium measure. We prove that $\widehatμ^{\sup}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ serves as a detector of arithmetic positivity. In particular, we determine it for weakly nef Hermitian line bundles and, in full generality, in the toric case. Moreover, $\widehatμ^{\sup}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ governs the arithmetic volume function and its variational properties. More precisely, we establish weak integral representations of the arithmetic volume and of its first variation in terms of $\widehatμ^{\sup}_{\mathrm{eq}}$. These formulas provide a measure-theoretic interpretation of the volume derivative of Yuan--Zhang and reveal a structural parallel with variational formulas in complex pluripotential theory. Our approach differs fundamentally from previous treatments based on Harder--Narasimhan filtrations or arithmetic Okounkov bodies. It places the arithmetic volume at the center of the theory and suggests that asymptotic volume invariants, rather than heights, provide the natural analytic framework for problems in equidistribution and arithmetic dynamics.

Rigid analytic 1-motives and conjugate uniformization of abeloid varieties

Khai-Hoan Nguyen-Dang, Xu Shen, Heer Zhao

Abeloid varieties are the p-adic analogs of complex abelian varieties (higher-dimensional generalizations of elliptic curves), living over p-adic fields. This paper develops a theory of 'rigid analytic 1-motives'—algebraic objects encoding how these varieties degenerate—and establishes a p-adic conjugate uniformization theorem, providing a powerful new framework for understanding p-adic geometry analogous to classical results over the complex numbers.

math.NT math.AG
Original abstract

Let $K$ be a $p$-adic field. We study the arithmetic theory of abeloid varieties over $K$. Our aims are twofold. First, we study the theory of rigid analytic 1-motives, which will be viewed as a tool to describe degeneration of abeloid varieties, similarly as in the classical algebraic setting. Our key new results are the equivalence between formal (resp. log formal) 1-motives over $\mathcal{O}_K$ and rigid analytic 1-motives with good (resp. semi-stable) reduction over $K$, and the Néron-Ogg-Shafarevich criterion for the good (resp. semi-stable) reduction of rigid analytic 1-motives. In particular, we construct log formal 1-motives and log $p$-divisible groups over $\mathcal{O}_K$ from semi-stable abeloid varieties over $K$. Next, we study the conjugate uniformization of an arbitrary abeloid variety $A$ over $K$. This is a type of $p$-adic uniformization initiated by Iovita--Morrow--Zaharescu in case of abelian varieties with good reduction. Our approach here is based on Fargues' theory of $p$-divisible rigid analytic groups. In fact, we view the theory of conjugate uniformization as a study of rational points of dualizable $p$-divisible rigid analytic groups in terms of their classification Hodge--Tate triples. Along the way, we construct $p$-divisible rigid analytic groups from rigid analytic 1-motives.

An elliptic K3 surface X/Q(t) with Mordell-Weil rank 17, I: Formulas for X and base changes of rank 18 and 19

Noam D. Elkies

An elliptic K3 surface is a geometric object foliated by elliptic curves, and its Mordell-Weil rank measures how many independent rational points its generic fiber carries. This paper provides explicit formulas for a surface achieving the maximum possible Mordell-Weil rank of 17 over Q(t), announced by Elkies nearly two decades ago, plus base changes reaching ranks 18 and 19. One fiber was the highest-rank known elliptic curve from 2006 to 2024.

math.NT
Original abstract

In [Elkies 2006, Elkies 2007] we announced a K3 surface X/Q with Néron--Severi group NS(X) = NS_Q(X) of rank 19 and an elliptic fibration of Mordell--Weil rank 17. This is the largest possible Mordell--Weil rank over Q(t) for an elliptic K3 surface. One of the fibers of this surface is the elliptic curve of rank at least 28 that was the elliptic curve of highest rank known during the years 2006--2024. We further announced that there are quadratic base changes of this fibration to elliptic surfaces of Mordell--Weil rank 18 over Q(t), and that there are pairs of such quadratic base changes whose compositum is an elliptic surface of Mordell--Weil rank 19 over an elliptic curve E_0/Q with #(E_0(Q)) = \infty, whence there are infinitely many elliptic curves of rank at least 19 over Q. We exhibit one such pair. A subsequent paper will show (using a technique also announced in [Elkies 2007] how we computed X and its fibration.

Well and badly approximable sets, and rapid winning

Mumtaz Hussain, David Simmons

Badly approximable numbers are reals that resist rational approximation beyond a certain threshold, while τ-approximable numbers are those unusually well-approximated by rationals—these sets seem opposites, yet both are large in different senses. This paper computes the Hausdorff dimension (a fractal measure of size) of their intersection by inventing a new tool called the Ψ-rapid game, a refinement of a recent game-theoretic framework. The result resolves a natural but previously inaccessible question in metric number theory.

math.NT math.DS math.MG
Original abstract

The set of $τ$-approximable numbers, $\mathcal W(τ)$, has genuinely fractional Hausdorff dimension, whereas the set of inhomogeneously badly approximable numbers, $\Bad^γ$, has full Hausdorff dimension. We determine the Hausdorff dimension of their intersection by introducing the $Ψ$-rapid game, a scale-sensitive refinement of the rapid game of Hatefi and Simmons (preprint 2024). For every approximation function $ψ$, we prove that $\mathcal W(ψ)\cap\Bad^γ$ is strong $Ψ$-rapid winning for a natural gauge $Ψ$ determined by $ψ$. Unlike Schmidt-type games, whose winning property always implies full Hausdorff dimension, the $Ψ$-rapid game is calibrated to a prescribed Diophantine scale, so that the resulting dimension bound depends explicitly on the decay of $Ψ$. In particular, for $ψ(q)=q^{-τ}, τ\ge1,$ we recover the exact Jarník--Besicovitch dimension, that is, $$ \HD\bigl(\mathcal W(τ)\cap\Bad^γ\bigr)=\frac{2}{τ+1}.$$

Simultaneous Diophantine approximation on the three-dimensional Veronese curve: the complete Hausdorff dimension story

Dmitry Badziahin, Nikita Shulga

How well can points on the curve (t, t², t³) be approximated by rationals? This paper completes the Hausdorff dimension formula — a measure of the 'size' of exceptional sets — for the set of λ-well-approximable points on this three-dimensional Veronese curve, for all relevant λ. Notably, this makes the Veronese curve the first non-planar curve where the full Hausdorff dimension story in simultaneous Diophantine approximation is resolved.

math.NT
Original abstract

We determine the Hausdorff dimension of the intersection of the set $\mathcal W_3(λ)$ of simultaneously $λ$-well approximable points with the Veronese curve $\mathcal V_3 \subset \mathbb R^3$ for $λ\ge3/5$, thus completing the full range of $λ$ values. Precisely, we show that for $λ\ge 1/3$, $$ \dim\bigl(\mathcal W_3(λ)\cap\mathcal V_3\bigr)= \max\left\{\frac{2-2λ}{1+λ}, \frac{2}{3(1+λ)}\right\}. $$ To the best of the authors' knowledge, this makes $\mathcal V_3$ the first nondegenerate, non-planar curve with a completely determined Hausdorff dimension theory.

Pseudonullity for fine Selmer groups over multiple $\mathbb{Z}_p^{d}$-extensions

Peikai Qi, Ruichen Xu

Fine Selmer groups are arithmetic objects attached to elliptic curves and modular forms that encode deep number-theoretic information, and Coates–Sujatha conjectured they should be 'pseudonull' (vanishingly small in a precise algebraic sense) over large p-adic field extensions. This paper proves new cases of those conjectures for elliptic curves, cyclotomic characters, and families of modular forms, advancing our understanding of Iwasawa theory in multiple variables.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this article, we develop vertical control theorems for fine Selmer groups associated with Galois representations and their deformations in $p$-adic families. We apply them to provide new evidence for Conjectures A and B of Coates--Sujatha and to obtain pseudonullity of the fine Selmer groups for cyclotomic characters, elliptic curves, classical cuspidal newforms, and Hida families of $p$-ordinary cuspidal newforms.

The divisor function for matrices

Tim Browning, Nikita P. Kalinin, Alina Ostafe et al.

How many ways can you factor an integer matrix? This paper defines a matrix analog of the classical divisor function—counting factorizations AB=M where A and B are integer matrices with entries bounded by T—and proves asymptotic formulas and sharp upper bounds as T grows. The results extend classical number-theoretic ideas about counting divisors to the richer, non-commutative setting of matrix rings, where factorizations are far more complex.

math.NT
Original abstract

We introduce a matrix divisor function $τ_n(T,M)$, counting factorisations $AB=M$ for $n\times n$ integer matrices $A,B$ of height at most $T$. For a fixed non-singular $M$, or for the zero matrix $M=O_n$, we prove an asymptotic formula for $τ_n(T,M)$, as $T\to \infty$, using lattice point counting. We also prove an essentially sharp uniform upper bound for $τ_n(T,M)$, for an arbitrary non-singular matrix $M$.

The uniform Littlewood conjecture fails on a set of positive Hausdorff dimension

Nikita Shulga

The uniform Littlewood conjecture asks whether a certain product of fractional parts—measuring how well multiples of two numbers approximate integers simultaneously—must approach zero for all real pairs. Though true for almost all pairs, this paper proves counterexamples form a set of positive Hausdorff dimension (a fractal-like set with non-negligible size), significantly strengthening a recent result that found only a topologically large but measure-zero set of exceptions.

math.NT
Original abstract

The uniform Littlewood conjecture (ULC), introduced by Bandi, Fregoli and Kleinbock, asserts in the two-number case that $$ \lim_{Q\to\infty} Q\min_{1\le n\le Q}\|nξ\|\,\|nζ\|=0 $$ for all real $ξ,ζ$. It is proven to hold for almost every pair $(ξ,ζ)$. Schleischitz, however, has recently disproved the full statement and showed that the set of counterexamples contains a dense $G_δ$ set. We prove that a set of counterexample pairs with the first coordinate being a badly approximable number has Hausdorff dimension at least $3/2$. We further show that the set of badly approximable numbers $ξ$ for which there exists $ζ$ such that $(ξ,ζ)$ is a counterexample to ULC has full Hausdorff dimension. This contrasts with the classical Littlewood conjecture, for which the set of possible counterexamples is known to have Hausdorff dimension $0$.

Oscillation of partial sums of the Möbius function and zeros of Riemann's zeta function

János Pintz

The Mertens function M(x) sums the Möbius function—a number-theoretic function encoding prime factorization structure—up to x, and its oscillation is deeply tied to the Riemann zeta function's zeros. This paper strengthens earlier results showing the average size of |M(x)| grows without bound, making the connection between this growth and the distribution of zeta zeros more explicit. The work advances understanding of how prime distribution controls the long-term behavior of M(x).

math.NT
Original abstract

The oscillation of M(x), the partial sum of the Möbius function has been in the focus of researchers in the theory of primes since the famous conjecture of Mertens in 1905 (formulated in a weaker form by Stieltjes in 1885 in a letter to Hermite). The average order of the modulus of M(x) in an interval of type [0,Y] is clearly in connection with the distribution of primes. The author proved at the beginning of 1980's that this average tends unconditionally to infinity with Y. The present work shows that (similarly to the average order of the error term of the Prime Number Theorem) this average agrees with great accuracy for large values of Y with the largest error term of the Riemann-von Mangoldt prime number formula for the value Y.

Metaconjugation and Quadratic Forms

Adriana Cardoso, António Leite, António Machiavelo et al.

Which quadratic forms represent every positive integer? This paper gives a quaternion-algebra proof that t²+2x²+5y²+10z² hits all positive integers, and characterizes the exceptions for t²+x²+7y²+7z². The key innovation is a new technique called 'metaconjugation' applied to quaternions, offering a unified algebraic framework that could streamline proofs of similar representation theorems.

math.NT
Original abstract

We present a quaternionic proof that the quadratic form $t^2+2x^2+5y^2+10z^2$ represents all positive integers, and that the form $t^2+x^2+7y^2+7z^2$ represents all natural numbers which are neither equal to $3 \cdot 7^\ell$ nor equal to $6 \cdot 7^\ell$, for any $\ell \in \mathbb{N}_0$. To do this we introduce a technique that may be useful for other purposes, and that we call "metaconjugation".

Integral quadratic forms over a ring of $p$-adic integers

Mrunal Hardikar, Anuradha S. Garge

A quadratic form is called universal over a ring if it represents every element of that ring. This paper extends known results about universality of diagonal quadratic forms from integer matrices to p-adic integer matrices, establishing necessary and sufficient conditions and sharpening bounds on how many variables are needed. The work builds on Lee's 2018 framework and advances our understanding of quadratic forms over non-commutative rings.

math.NT math.RA
Original abstract

Jungin Lee in 2018 proved a necessary and sufficient condition that an integral quadratic form $\sum_{i=1}^{m} a_iX_i^2$ is universal over $M_2(\mathbb{Z})$. For a positive integer $n \geq 2$, Lee defined $f(n)$ to be the smallest positive integer $m$ such that for every pairwise coprime $a_1, a_2, \ldots a_m \in \mathbb{Z}$, $\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_iX_i^2$ is universal over $M_n(\mathbb{Z})$. He gave bounds on $f(n)$ too. Koo and Lee further improved the bounds in 2025. This paper is organized as follows. Let $\mathbb{Z}_p$ be the ring of $p$-adic integers. In the first section we give necessary and sufficient condition for a quadratic form $\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_iX_i^2$ to be universal over $M_2(\mathbb{Z}_p)$ for $p=2$ and for an odd prime $p$. Consequently we express matrices of over $\mathbb{Z}_p$ as sum of squares. For a positive integer $n \geq 3$, we define $f(n)$ to be the smallest positive integer $m$ such that for every $p$-adic integers $a_1,a_2, \ldots a_m$ with at least three of them units, the diagonal quadratic form $\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_iX_i^2$ is universal over $M_n(\mathbb{Z}_p)$. In the next section we find bounds on $f(n)$ for $n \geq 3$.

Contributions to the theory of the Euler eta function

Torre Caparatta, Sebastian Pauli, Filip Saidak

The Euler eta function (a close cousin of the Riemann zeta function, built from an alternating Dirichlet series) has zeros whose locations matter deeply for number theory. This paper studies where those zeros fall for both ordinary and fractional-order derivatives of the eta function. Understanding zero distributions helps clarify the analytic structure of these functions and connects to broader questions about L-functions.

math.NT
Original abstract

We investigate the distribution of the zeros of the Euler's function and its integral and fractional derivatives.

Permutation and exceptionality of rational functions from equivariant elliptic isogenies over finite fields

Xiang Fan

When an elliptic curve isogeny (a structure-preserving map between elliptic curves) is equivariant under a group symmetry, it induces a rational function on projective lines. This paper proves that such functions never accidentally permute the points of any finite field extension — a property called exceptionality — unless forced to by the underlying algebraic structure. This rules out a whole family of candidates for exceptional permutation polynomials.

math.NT math.AG
Original abstract

Let $k=\mathbb F_q$ with a fixed algebraic closure $\bar k$. Let $E,E'/k$ be elliptic curves with faithful origin-preserving actions of a nontrivial finite group $Λ$, and let $\varphi:E\to E'$ be a separable $Λ$-equivariant $k$-isogeny. Assume that the prescribed action on $E$ is stable under $q$-power Frobenius conjugation, and let $f$ be the induced map between the quotient lines. We prove that this family has no accidental permutations: for every $k_r=\mathbb F_{q^r}$, the map $f$ permutes $\mathbf P^1(k_r)$ if and only if it is exceptional over $k_r$. More precisely, writing $\mathcal N=\ker\varphi(\bar k)$, these equivalent properties are controlled entirely by the induced Frobenius and $Λ$-actions on $\mathcal N$: they hold if and only if \[ \mathcal N\cap\ker(\mathcal F_E^r-γ_E)=\{\mathrm O_E\} \qquad\text{for all }γ\inΛ. \] Here $\mathrm O_E$ is the origin of $E$, $\mathcal F_E$ is $q$-power Frobenius, and $γ_E$ denotes the action of $γ$ on $E$. A weighted Frobenius-sector identity makes the criterion exact even over ramified quotient fibres. We identify the geometric and arithmetic monodromy groups as affine groups on $\mathcal N$ and prove that the permutation support is purely periodic, with explicit least positive period and natural density. For multiplication maps the criterion reduces to determinant tests on torsion, yielding explicit split and nonsplit criteria for cyclic quotient groups of orders $2$, $3$, $4$, and $6$.

On the Geometry and Shapes of Rank 2 Log Unit Lattices

Jose Cruz, Erik Holmes, Fatemeh Jalalvand et al.

Every number field (a finite extension of the rationals) produces a 'log unit lattice' by taking logarithms of its units, and the shape of this lattice encodes arithmetic information. While integer ring lattice shapes are well-studied, unit shapes are far less understood. This paper gives the first detailed analysis for rank-2 cases, characterizing where unit shapes sit in the moduli space of lattice shapes and connecting them to the field's arithmetic invariants.

math.NT
Original abstract

Every number field canonically gives rise to two lattices: its ring of integers and its log unit lattice. While the shapes of the former have undergone extensive research, far less is known about the shapes of the latter, referred to as unit shapes. This paper presents an in-depth analysis of the unit shapes of number fields with unit rank 2. Our first main result characterizes, in many cases, the location of unit shapes within the fundamental domain of the space of rank 2 lattice shapes in terms of the Galois group of the field's Galois closure, and determines when these unit shapes are transcendental. Next, we establish that the unit shape uniquely determines the field up to isomorphism for totally imaginary $D_6$ non-CM sextic fields; this result fails in the CM case. Finally, for certain subfamilies of $D_6$ non-CM imaginary sextics, we offer a simple sufficient condition for their log unit lattices to be orthogonal and provide lower bounds on the proportion of fields with orthogonal log unit lattice.

Fourier Coefficients of the Degenerate Eisenstein Series on Symplectic Groups

Alan Xuelun Hou, Yusheng Lei

Eisenstein series are automorphic forms built by inducing representations from subgroups of algebraic groups like symplectic groups. This paper shows that a specific Fourier coefficient of a degenerate Eisenstein series on a symplectic group, when restricted to a smaller symplectic group, is again an Eisenstein series with the same parameter—enabling a new 'automorphic descent' construction. This gives a concrete tool for transferring automorphic representations between groups, a central goal in the Langlands program.

math.NT math.RT
Original abstract

We study degenerate Eisenstein series on symplectic groups and construct a new automorphic descent. We show that a certain Fourier coefficient, after restriction to a smaller symplectic group, is itself an Eisenstein series with the same inducing parameter \(s\). We describe the resulting holomorphic section explicitly in terms of the original section and local \(L\)-factors at unramified places. At every regular point of the local descent, we prove surjectivity at non-Archimedean places and dense image at Archimedean places. We finally indicate an application of the iterated descent to maximal Fourier coefficients of Eisenstein series.

Infinite continued fractions with a finite alphabet and their applications

Mykola Pratsiovytyi, Sofiia Ratushniak

If you build infinite continued fractions using only digits drawn from a fixed finite set, what does the collection of all resulting real numbers look like? This paper shows that set is always bounded, uncountably infinite, and perfect (closed with no isolated points—like a Cantor set). It then identifies exactly when the set is a full interval versus a fractal, with applications to number theory and the geometry of real numbers.

math.NT
Original abstract

The paper investigates the topological and metric properties of the set $E$ of values of all infinite continued fractions of the form \[0+\frac{1}{a_1+\dfrac{1}{a_2+_{\ddots}}}=1/a_1+1/a_2+...+1/a_n+...\equiv[0;a_1,a_2,...,a_n,...],\] whose partial quotients ($a_n$) take values in a finite set of positive real numbers $\{e_0,e_1,...,e_{s-1}\}$, $e_0<e_1<...<e_{s-1}$. It is established that the set $E$ is bounded, has cardinality continuum, and is perfect. Conditions under which $E$ is an interval, a nowhere dense set, or a set of Lebesgue measure zero are established. Necessary and sufficient conditions are obtained for $E$ to be an interval and for the corresponding coding system by such continued fractions to have zero redundancy, i.e., for each number to have at most two representations. The geometric meaning of the digits in this representation, as well as the metric relations, is described in terms of the properties of cylindrical sets (cylinders and cylindrical intervals). It is proved that the basic metric ratio, defined as the ratio of the diameter of a cylinder to the diameter of its parent cylinder, is bounded away from both zero and one.

A Buium--Coleman bound for the Zilber--Pink conjecture for curves inside abelian varieties

Netan Dogra, Sudip Pandit

The Zilber–Pink conjecture predicts that a curve in an abelian variety (a higher-dimensional torus-like algebraic object) meets only finitely many 'special' subvarieties—a vast generalization of classical results like Mordell's conjecture. This paper gives an explicit bound on how large that finite intersection can be, using techniques inspired by Buium and Coleman involving reduction modulo primes. It also delivers a new, self-contained proof of finiteness itself.

math.NT math.AG
Original abstract

We provide an explicit bound for the image of the Zilber--Pink locus of a curve in an abelian variety under reduction modulo a suitably large prime $p$. We also provide a new proof of finiteness of the Zilber--Pink locus. Under further assumptions on the abelian variety, we prove a bound on the size of the locus.

Fourier Spectral Reciprocity and Canonical Hecke $L$-Functions

Liyang Yang

Spectral reciprocity formulas relate two different ways of averaging L-functions, powerful tools for understanding prime distribution and arithmetic. This paper proves a new Fourier-type reciprocity over number fields, then applies it to compute explicit first-moment formulas—averages of central values and derivatives—for Hecke L-functions over CM fields (number fields with a complex conjugation symmetry), with uniformity across the key parameters.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove a Fourier-type toric spectral reciprocity formula over number fields, relating Tate integrals over a quadratic extension to a dual family of Hecke periods over the base field. The formula keeps the spectral parameter free and allows test functions adapted to arithmetic applications. We apply this reciprocity formula to canonical Hecke $L$-functions over CM fields and obtain explicit first moment formulas, including both central values and central derivatives. These formulas are uniform in the weight and the twisting conductor, and require no Heegner-type splitting hypothesis. Together with subconvexity bounds, they yield quantitative nonvanishing results for canonical Hecke $L$-functions and their derivatives. In weight one, this gives a rank-one analogue of the arithmetic applications of Masri--Yang, yielding quantitative Mordell--Weil rank-one results for quadratic twists of the associated CM abelian varieties.

The equality cases $P_t(\mathbb{N})=\tfrac12$ for the deconvolved sum-of-digits measures

Dawid Tarłowski

When you count ones in binary representations, you get surprisingly structured probability measures. This paper identifies exactly when a certain 'deconvolved' measure—built from how binary digit sums change when you add t—assigns probability exactly 1/2 to the natural numbers. The result settles specific equality cases in a recursive tree structure introduced recently, sharpening our understanding of the fractal-like arithmetic of binary digit sums.

math.NT math.CO math.PR
Original abstract

Let $s(n)$ denote the number of ones in the binary expansion of an integer $n\in\mathbb{N}$, and let $μ_t$ be the probability measure on $\mathbb{Z}$ defined by the asymptotic densities of the level sets of the function $\mathbb{N}\ni n\mapsto s(n+t)-s(n)\in\mathbb{Z}$. Let $P_t$ be the family of finitely supported measures defined by the convolution $μ_t=μ_1*P_t$. Recently, Tarlowski (2026) has shown that the family $P_t$ may be represented as a recursively grown binary tree $T_t$, and that the Cusick's conjecture - $μ_t(\mathbb{N})>\frac12$, $t\in\mathbb{N}$, - follows from the asymmetry property of the family $T_t$, which was posed there as an open problem. Next, Cheng (2026) has provided the combinatorial description of the family $T_t$ in the language of principal subsequence ideals, and proved both conjectures. Both of these problems are directly related to the problem of determining the zeros of the function $\mathbb{N}\ni t \mapsto P_t(\mathbb{N})-\frac12\in[0,\tfrac12]$, a problem left open by Cheng (2026) as a saturation problem, and previously analyzed only numerically. In this paper we solve this problem completely. Writing an odd integer $t\ge3$ as $t=(1\,w\,1)_2$ with $w\in\{0,1\}^{\star}$, we show that $P_t(\mathbb{N})=\frac12$ if and only if $w$ is \emph{saturated} in the following sense: in the block decomposition $w=1^{a_0}\,0\,1^{a_1}\,0\cdots0\,1^{a_k}$ with exactly $k$ zeros, every block of "1" satisfies $a_i\ge k$. Additionally, we show that the lower bound for $P_t(\mathbb{N})$ established by Cheng for $0$-initial words holds true for all non-saturated words.

A deterministic sin^2-type algorithm for complex cubic irrationalities with exact periodicity certificates

Ludovic Tagnon

Hermite asked in 1848 for a number representation whose periodicity detects cubic irrationals (roots of degree-3 polynomials), analogous to how continued fractions detect quadratic irrationals. This paper solves the complex cubic case using a deterministic version of Karpenkov's sin²-algorithm, proving the algorithm terminates with exact periodicity certificates. It closes a major open problem in multi-dimensional continued fraction theory.

math.NT
Original abstract

Hermite asked in 1848 for a representation of real numbers whose eventual periodicity characterizes cubic irrationals. The totally real case was solved by Karpenkov's $\sin^2$-algorithm; the complex case, signature (1,1), is his Problem 4. We study a deterministic algorithm implementing his suggested analytic extension: the score expression is strictly negative on (1,1) data (closed form proved), the most negative score is selected, and exact score ties are resolved by a declared ordering. On a sample of 205 complex cubic polynomials, every run closes projectively with an exact unit certificate, each transition certified by exact comparisons in $\mathbb{Q}(α)$. An exhaustive campaign over the full box $[-3,3]^3$ closes 194/194. Across 457 deformed bases, the terminal cycle is an invariant of the marked lattice. Certified finite transition graphs are computed for four fields; the plastic case is machine-checked in Lean 4, kernel-only. All data ship in a public archive with a portable verifier.

On the binary representation of powers of $3$

Yann Bugeaud

This paper proves that only finitely many powers of 3 have a 'simple' binary representation—meaning their base-2 expansion has few 1-digits. It addresses a long-standing question about how powers of one prime can be structured in another base, connecting number theory and digital properties of integers.

math.NT
Original abstract

We establish that only finitely many powers of $3$ can have a simple binary representation.

Jacob's ladders, new classes of sum variants of almost linear formula and corresponding $ζ$-functionals and $ζ$-equivalents of the Fermat-Wiles theorem

Jan Moser

Using 'Jacob's ladders'—special functions tied to the Riemann zeta function—this paper constructs new families of zeta-functionals and arithmetic identities analogous in structure to Fermat's Last Theorem. The constructions rely on rectangular signals indexed by Gram points, specific real numbers where the zeta function takes real values.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this paper we obtain new classes of the second generation of new $ζ$-functionals and corresponding $ζ$-equivalents of the Fermat-Wiles theorem. These are based on sets of elementary rectangular $ζ$-signals generated by the Gramm's sequence.

The Period-Three Secondary Term in the Mean Value of $L(1/2,χ_D)$ in the Hyperelliptic Ensemble

Hwanyup Jung

When averaging L-functions (number-theoretic objects encoding arithmetic data) over quadratic characters on function fields, Florea found an asymptotic formula with a secondary term — but this paper shows that formula was incomplete. Two additional complex conjugate poles contribute at the same order as the previously known real pole, adding an oscillatory 'period-three' correction. Correcting such moment formulas matters for understanding the statistical distribution of L-function values.

math.NT
Original abstract

Let $q$ be an odd prime power. We revisit Florea's asymptotic formula for the first moment of quadratic Dirichlet $L$-functions over the odd-degree hyperelliptic ensemble $\mathcal H_{2g+1}$, and show that its secondary term is not the complete one. The square-dual generating function has three double poles of the same modulus on the secondary circle: the positive real one reproduces Florea's polynomial, while the two nonreal conjugate poles contribute at the same order. The complete secondary term is $q^{2g/3}(α_{g\bmod3}g+β_{g\bmod3})$, with real coefficients whose dependence on the genus has minimal period exactly three, and on at least two of the three residue classes the nonreal poles contribute a term of order $gq^{2g/3}$. We also prove the formula for every odd prime power $q$, whereas Florea assumes $q\equiv1\bmod4$ throughout; this is achieved by a phase-normalized quadratic Gauss sum that restores the multiplicativity and the local evaluations her argument uses. Exact finite-ensemble moments for $q=3$ exhibit the three residue-class trends.

On the constant in Klartag's theorem for lattice packings in high dimensions

Irina Rezvyakova

Klartag's 2025 breakthrough showed lattice sphere packings—arrangements of non-overlapping equal spheres centered at grid points—can achieve density at least cn²·2⁻ⁿ in n dimensions. This paper sharpens the result by identifying the best possible constant: c = 1/(2e). Since sphere packing density bounds are notoriously hard to pin down, making an asymptotically optimal constant explicit is a meaningful refinement of a landmark result.

math.NT
Original abstract

In 2025, Boaz Klartag proved that there exists a lattice sphere packing in $\mathbb{R}^n$ whose density is at least \[ c n^2 2^{-n}, \] where $c>0$ is an absolute constant. Our aim is to refine the analysis of Klartag's argument and to show that the constant can be taken as \[ c = \frac{1}{2e} - o(1), \qquad n\to\infty. \]

Transcendence of Tonelli--Shanks Power Modulo Infinitely Large Primes

Tomoki Mihara

The Tonelli–Shanks algorithm computes modular square roots—solutions to x²≡n mod p—using a specific exponent depending on the prime p. This paper proves that certain sequences built from these exponents are transcendental over the rationals when interpreted in the Arithmetic ring 𝒜 (a ring capturing number-theoretic behavior across infinitely many primes simultaneously). The result reveals unexpected irrationality in a purely algorithmic quantity, connecting computational number theory to transcendence theory.

math.NT
Original abstract

For a prime number $p$, we denote by $k_p$ the greatest odd divisor of $p-1$. Tonelli--Shanks algorithm is a classical algorithm to compute a square root of a quadratic residue $n \in \mathbb{Z}$ modulo $p$ as the product of $n^{\frac{k_p+1}{2}}$, which we call {\it Tonelli--Shanks power of $n$}, and a correction term given as a power of a quadratic non-residue modulo $p$. We prove the transcendence over $\mathbb{Q}$ of the images of the following in the ring $\mathscr{A}$ of integers modulo infinitely large primes: the greatest odd divisor $k_p$ of $p-1$, the composite $f(\frac{p - 1}{k_p})$ of any injective map $f \colon \mathbb{Z} \to \mathbb{Z}$ and the greatest $2$-power $\frac{p - 1}{k_p}$ dividing $p-1$, Tonelli--Shanks power $n^{\frac{_p+1}{2}}$ of any $n \in \mathbb{Z} \setminus \{-1,0,1\}$, and the correction term of Tonelli--Shanks algorithm for such $n$.

Algebraic Geometry

43

Towards the Global Torelli Theorem

Daniil Serebrennikov

The Global Torelli Theorem asks when Hodge-theoretic data (how differential forms behave) completely determines a variety's geometry. This paper proves finiteness results for K-trivial varieties (those with trivial canonical bundle, like K3 surfaces and Calabi-Yau manifolds) in all dimensions, establishing conditions under which Hodge theory forces fibers of a family to be isomorphic. This pushes a celebrated surface-level theorem toward a long-sought higher-dimensional generalization.

math.AG
Original abstract

In this paper, we prove finiteness results for K-trivial varieties in all dimensions. In dimension 2, these results are corollaries of the Global Torelli Theorem for polarized K3 surfaces. In particular, we establish a sufficient condition when two fibers of a projective family of K-trivial varieties are isomorphic via the Hodge theory. As applications, we prove that if a projective family of K-trivial varieties has a dense subset of birationally equivalent fibers, then all fibers are isomorphic.

Disjoint and nearly disjoint sums of matrix multiplication tensors and their centroids

Martin Kassabov, J. M. Landsberg, Victor Souza et al.

The centroid of a tensor is an invariant algebra capturing its symmetries, and it was widely conjectured to always be small. This paper shatters that belief by constructing explicit tensors—built from matrix multiplication tensors—with unexpectedly large centroids, and proves a nearly tight upper bound on centroid dimension. As a bonus, the authors develop a geometric method using centroids to produce border rank decompositions, with direct implications for understanding the complexity of matrix multiplication.

math.AG cs.CC math.AC math.RA
Original abstract

This paper addresses centroids, which are fundamental invariants of tensors. Our main results are as follows: (i) The construction of explicit tensors with very large centroids, whereas previously it had been conjectured that none such exist. (ii) An upper bound on the dimension of the centroid that is essentially attained by our examples. (iii) The development of a geometric technique to write down border rank decomposition of tensors using centroids and "extended centroids". (iv) The technique is applied to tensors of this paper to prove they are of minimal border rank. The technique is versatile and enables us to geometrically derive and improve upon previous ad hoc decompositions. (v) The construction of symmetric tensors with large centroids and proof that they are wild in the sense of Buczyńska-Buczyński. Our results also pave the way for new upper bounds on the exponent of matrix multiplication. The geometric technique also constructs new "better" tensors for Strassen's laser method from old, and we apply this to the tensors of Strassen and Schönhage to get better tensors in the sense that they give better upper bounds on the exponent than the original tensors.

Toric vector bundles and the number of zeros of vertical systems

Carles Checa

When does a parametrized polynomial system achieve the maximum possible number of complex solutions? This paper gives necessary and sufficient conditions by attaching a toric vector bundle — a geometric object encoding how solutions vary — to each 'vertical' system, a structured family where coefficients vary in specific directions. The result bridges algebraic geometry and polynomial system solving, offering sharp solution-count criteria relevant to applications in kinematics and computational algebra.

math.AG
Original abstract

We provide necessary and sufficient conditions for the specializations of a vertically parametrized polynomial system to attain the maximal number of complex nonzero solutions. To each vertical system, we attach a pair consisting of a projective simplicial toric variety and a toric vector bundle. The toric vector bundle allows for a homogenization of the polynomials in the Cox ring of the toric variety, providing a homogeneous ideal with the same zeros over the torus as the original system. We show that the maximal number of isolated solutions is attained if and only if this ideal has no solutions in the faces of the toric variety and prove that this happens for generic values of the parameters. In addition, we provide a novel formula for this generic number of zeros as an alternating sum of mixed volumes over a family of polytopes attached to the toric vector bundle.

Geometric Invariant Theory of Peterson Varieties

Arkadev Ghosh, Santosha Pattanayak

Peterson varieties are special subvarieties of flag manifolds (spaces parameterizing nested sequences of subspaces) that arise in combinatorics and representation theory. This paper analyzes their GIT quotients—a geometric way to divide out group symmetries—explicitly describing which points are 'stable' using combinatorial data from root systems. The results clarify the chamber structure governing how these quotients change as parameters vary.

math.AG
Original abstract

We study the GIT quotients of the Peterson variety $\mathrm{Pet}_n\subset \mathrm{GL}(n,\mathbb C)/B$ under a one-parameter subgroup $λ:\mathbb G_m \to T$ with respect to the linearization $\mathcal L(χ)$ given by a regular dominant character $χ$ in the root lattice. Using the Richardson stratification, we describe the semistable and stable loci explicitly in terms of subsets of simple roots. This determines the GIT chamber decomposition and the corresponding wall-crossing morphisms. In the deep chamber, the quotient is shown to be isomorphic to the weighted projective space $\mathbb P(1,2,\ldots,n-1)$. We obtain a complete chamber-theoretic characterization of normality and describe how normality varies with the choice of linearization. We also prove that the quotient is smooth if and only if $n\le3$, independently of the regular dominant linearization. These results describe how the singular geometry of the Peterson variety is reflected in the variation of its GIT quotients.

Kawamata--Miyaoka type inequality for weak Fano varieties

Haidong Liu

A weak Fano variety is an algebraic variety whose anticanonical divisor is nef and big, a mild positivity condition. This paper proves a Kawamata–Miyaoka-type inequality bounding the top self-intersection of the anticanonical class by lower-degree characteristic class terms, generalizing classical inequalities to this broader singular setting and providing new constraints on the geometry of such varieties.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $X$ be a $\mathbb Q$-factorial $ε$-lc weak Fano variety of dimension $n\geq 2$, where $0<ε\leq 1$ is a real number. Then there exists a Kawamata--Miyaoka type inequality \[ c_1(X)^n\leq \frac{2(1+ε)}ε\,\hat c_2(X)\cdot c_1(X)^{n-2}. \]

The Geometry of the Exceptional Component of Degree Two Foliations on $\mathbb{P}^3$

Claudia R. Alcántara, Dominique Cerveau

What does the space of all degree-two foliations—geometric structures that partition a space into lower-dimensional leaves—on projective 3-space look like near its boundary? This paper fully describes the boundary of one special connected component, showing it consists of exactly four irreducible pieces, each 12-dimensional. The result clarifies how "nice" foliations degenerate, advancing the classification of foliation spaces that has been a central goal in algebraic geometry.

math.AG
Original abstract

We study the exceptional component of the space $\mathbb{F}(2,\mathbb{P}^3)$, of codimension-one foliations of degree two on $\mathbb{P}^3$. We describe the geometry of its boundary and prove that it has four irreducible components, all of dimension $12$. Three of these components contain a dense subset given by the orbit of a logarithmic foliation of type $(1,1,2)$, while the fourth contains a family of pull-back type foliations from $\mathbb{P}^2$ whose orbits have dimension $11$.

Isocrystals on unibranch varieties and open immersions

Adrian Langer, Lei Zhang

When you restrict to an open subset of a nice geometric space, how much of the space's 'cohomological DNA' is lost? For overconvergent isocrystals—p-adic coefficient systems on varieties in positive characteristic—the answer is controlled: the restriction map between Tannaka duals (algebraic groups encoding the category) is surjective. Crucially, this fails for convergent isocrystals, with a counterexample built from Crew's construction, sharply distinguishing the two theories.

math.AG math.NT
Original abstract

We prove that for a connected, geometrically unibranch variety $X$ over a perfect field of positive characteristic and a dense open subset $U \subset X$, the canonical homomorphism between the Tannaka duals of the categories of overconvergent isocrystals is a quotient map. We also show that the analogous statement for convergent isocrystals, and for all isocrystals, fails. The counterexample is based on Crew's construction of a unit-root $F$-isocrystal that is convergent but not overconvergent.

Beauville--Bogomolov--Fujiki positivity and Moishezonness of complex symplectic manifolds in Fujiki class $\mathscr C$

Jian Chen

Hyperkähler manifolds carry a special quadratic form called the Beauville–Bogomolov–Fujiki (BBF) form, whose positivity is deeply tied to the manifold's geometry. This paper proves that for the broader class of hyperfujiki manifolds—bimeromorphic analogues of hyperkähler manifolds—being Moishezon (roughly, having enough meromorphic functions to behave like a projective variety) is equivalent to having a BBF-positive line bundle. This links two previously separate notions and extends core hyperkähler theory to a wider geometric setting.

math.AG math.CV math.SG
Original abstract

Motivated by the principle that positivity of the Beauville--Bogomolov--Fujiki (BBF) form controls the geometry of hyperkähler manifolds, we study BBF positivity from the perspective of bimeromorphic geometry, focusing on its relation to the Moishezonness of hyperfujiki manifolds (natural bimeromorphic analogues of hyperkähler manifolds). We prove that any Moishezon hyperfujiki manifold carries a big line bundle with positive BBF square. We also prove that the existence of a BBF-positive integral $(1,1)$-class implies Moishezonness for hyperfujiki $4$-folds and, more generally, for hyperfujiki $2n$-folds under a weak Kähler minimal-model condition. The proof mainly uses the theory of primitive symplectic varieties developed by B. Bakker--C. Lehn, a construction of small bimeromorphic models for certain $K$-trivial manifolds by I. Biswas--J. Cao--S. Dumitrescu--H. Guenancia, and the theory of pull-backs of reflexive differential forms established by S. Kebekus--C. Schnell. As an application, we give a Hodge-theoretic description of the Moishezon locus in certain smooth families by combining this criterion with the period theory developed by B. Anthes--A. Cattaneo--S. Rollenske--A. Tomassini.

On the geometry of Fano varieties with Lefschetz defect 2

Cinzia Casagrande

The Lefschetz defect of a Fano variety—a positively curved algebraic manifold—measures how far its topology deviates from a simple fiber bundle structure. Prior work classified Fano varieties with defect 3 or higher; this paper tackles defect exactly 2, proving that after a controlled sequence of birational surgeries called flips, any such variety admits a conic bundle structure (a fibration whose fibers are conics). This fills a key gap in the classification of Fano varieties by their topological complexity.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let X be a smooth, complex Fano variety, and delta(X) its Lefschetz defect. It is known that if delta(X) is at least 4, then X is isomorphic to a product SxT where S is a surface, whereas if delta(X)=3, X admits a del Pezzo fibration with a prescribed structure. In this paper, we study the case where delta(X)=2. Our first result is that there exists a sequence of flips X-->X' such that X' is smooth and admits a conic bundle structure X'->Y, with rho(X)-rho(Y)=2. The conic bundle factors as X'->X''->Y, where X'->X'' is a smooth blow-up, and f: X''->Y is again a conic bundle. Then we show that either f is smooth, or there exists a del Pezzo fibration g: X->T with rho(X)-rho(T)=3 and T smooth, admitting two possible generic fibers, and we describe the relative cone NE(g). Finally for one of the possible generic fibers we give a structure theorem for the del Pezzo fibration g; we show that T is Fano and that X can be reconstructed from T and a suitable divisor class on T. As an application, we construct a family of Fano 4-folds X exhibiting this behaviour.

The Multivariable Strong Monodromy Conjecture for Plane Curves

Sheng Tan

The Strong Monodromy Conjecture links two ways of measuring the singularities of a polynomial: its topological zeta function (from topology) and its Bernstein-Sato ideal (from algebra). This paper proves the multivariable version of this conjecture for plane curves, showing that poles of the zeta function must correspond to roots of the Bernstein-Sato ideal—a long-sought bridge between analytic and algebraic invariants of singularities.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $F=(f_1,\ldots,f_r)$ be a tuple of holomorphic germs on a smooth complex germ, and let $B_{F,0}$ be its Bernstein--Sato ideal. We develop an iterated-residue obstruction showing that a nonzero coefficient-valued residue class on an SNC stratum forces the corresponding exact affine parameter to lie in $Z(B_{F,0})$. As applications, we prove that every maximal-order polar hyperplane of the local multivariable topological zeta function is contained in the Bernstein--Sato zero locus, and that the same holds for every actual polar hyperplane associated with a tuple of reduced plane curve germs. The latter proves the topological multivariable Strong Monodromy Conjecture for plane curves.

Negative Effective Divisors and Bridgeland Stability of Line Bundles on Surfaces

Anthony Mäkelä

When is a line bundle on a surface 'stable' in the sense of Bridgeland—a notion central to modern algebraic geometry and mirror symmetry? This paper proves the Arcara–Miles conjecture, showing that instability of a line bundle is always witnessed by a concrete geometric object: a negative-self-intersection effective divisor. The result gives a clean, checkable criterion and tightens our understanding of stability conditions on surfaces.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $X$ be a connected smooth complex projective surface. We prove an effective-divisor version of the Arcara--Miles conjecture, together with its strict analogue. For every divisorial Bridgeland stability condition, failure of stability, respectively semistability, of a line bundle or its relevant shift is detected by a natural subobject associated with a non-zero effective Cartier divisor $C$ satisfying $C^2<0$. The proof combines minimal-rank destabilizers, slope Harder--Narasimhan filtrations, the Bogomolov--Gieseker inequality, and an ordered Lorentzian partial-sum estimate that forces the minimal rank to be one. Consequently, strict semistability of a line bundle or its relevant shift is detected by a negative effective divisor, and a numerical semistability condition arising from the twisted deformed Hermitian--Yang--Mills equation is equivalent to stability under all integral scalings.

Generalised Cone Conjecture, I: Beyond Calabi--Yau

Vladimir Lazić, Isabel Stenger, Zhixin Xie

The Cone Conjecture predicts that certain symmetry groups acting on the space of curves or divisors on algebraic surfaces are 'almost periodic' — specifically, that they act with a finite fundamental domain. This paper proves a generalized version of this conjecture for surfaces beyond the Calabi-Yau setting (roughly, surfaces where the canonical bundle isn't trivial). It resolves a major open problem in algebraic geometry about the structure of nef cones.

math.AG
Original abstract

This is the first of two papers in which we prove the Generalised Cone Conjecture on surfaces.

Crepant partial resolutions of the nilpotent cone via Hamiltonian reduction

Gwyn Bellamy, Tom Gannon

The nilpotent cone — a central geometric object in Lie theory built from nilpotent elements of a semisimple algebraic group — has long been studied via its resolutions (spaces that smooth out its singularities). This paper shows that the nilpotent cone and all its crepant (singularity-preserving) partial resolutions arise uniformly from a construction called Hamiltonian reduction, a quotient process from symplectic geometry. This unifies previously scattered constructions and connects them to geometric invariant theory.

math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We show that the nilpotent cone associated to a simply connected semisimple algebraic group, together with all its crepant projective partial resolutions, can be constructed as Hamiltonian reductions of the affine closure of the cotangent bundle of base affine space for suitable choices of stability parameter of a maximal torus. We also realize the base change of the universal Poisson deformation of each of these crepant projective partial resolutions as the GIT quotient of the affine closure of the cotangent bundle of base affine space at the same stability parameter.

When are tropical multidegrees positive?

Yairon Cid-Ruiz

Tropical geometry replaces classical algebraic geometry with piecewise-linear combinatorial objects, and multidegrees measure how a tropical variety intersects coordinate hyperplanes in a product space. This paper identifies two combinatorial conditions — projection-purity and facet-selectability — that completely characterize when these intersection numbers are positive, showing the answer is governed by a polymatroid (a combinatorial structure generalizing matroids). This gives concrete tools for understanding the geometry of tropical varieties in product spaces.

math.AG math.CO
Original abstract

We study the positivity of the tropical multidegrees of a tropical variety contained in a product of real vector spaces. These multidegrees are obtained by stably intersecting the tropical variety with pullbacks of positive tropical divisors. We introduce projection-purity and facet-selectability, two conditions under which positivity is determined by the dimensions of the natural projections, and the support of the tropical multidegrees is precisely the set of lattice points of a polymatroid base polytope. This extends He's theorem for translation-admissible tropical varieties. We also show that these conditions alone do not force the corresponding tropical volume polynomial to be Lorentzian. By contrast, for the augmented Bergman fan of any polymatroid, the positive multidegrees are supported precisely on the lattice points of the polymatroid base polytope, and the tropical volume polynomial is Lorentzian for every sequence of positive tropical divisors.

Cyclic covers via mixed Hodge modules

Scott Hiatt

Cyclic covers are algebraic varieties built by taking an N-th root of a divisor (a geometric locus of zeros), and mixed Hodge modules are powerful algebraic tools encoding topological and analytic data simultaneously. This paper describes the pushforward of the structure sheaf under a cyclic cover entirely within Saito's mixed Hodge module framework, extending the classical theory to singular ambient varieties.

math.AG
Original abstract

Suppose we are given an arbitrary line bundle $\mathcal{L}$ on a complex algebraic variety $X$, not necessarily smooth. For a positive integer $N$, suppose there exists a global section $s \in Γ(X, \mathcal{L}^{N})$ that defines an effective Cartier divisor $D$. If we denote $π: Y \rightarrow X$ to be the $N$-fold cyclic covering of the divisor $D$ resulting from the global section $s$, we describe the cyclic covering in terms of Morihiko Saito's theory of mixed Hodge modules.
